&lt;project_context>
# 项目: faith-evidence-ie

## 运行环境
- 服务器名: rtx5090-4x
- SSH 账户: root（调用 ssh_execute / submit_training_job 时传入 account="root"）
- SSH 连接命令: ssh rtx5090-4x  # 必须用此命令，~/.ssh/config 自动应用密钥和跳板机，禁止用裸 IP
- 项目代码根目录: /root/autodl-tmp/freige


## 环境搭建
连接服务器后先调 `detect_image` 检测是否为预置镜像。如果匹配，跳过基础环境搭建，只装项目特有依赖。已预装的包不要重复安装。

## 输出风格
回复简洁直接。只输出执行结果和必要信息，不要解释过程、不要客套、不要给未被要求的建议。
&lt;/project_context>
根因排查 + 全面审计：为什么 3 个 eval 缺少 --sft_adapter？

**背景**:
- eval_plan_001_flat_nli_s43、eval_plan_012_dpo_ced_1ep、eval_plan_008_rsft_topk3 都缺少 --sft_adapter，导致 rel_f1≈0
- plan_011_bf16_g8_step_eval 正确传了 --sft_adapter，正常工作
- 早期的 mvp2_eval_rsft_r1 (CED s42) 也正常（rel_f1=0.4449）

**任务 1: 根因分析**
1. 读取 registry.yaml 中所有 eval 实验的 spec.command 字段
2. 对比成功 eval 和失败 eval 的命令差异
3. 检查 mvp2_eval_rsft_r1 (s42) 的 eval 命令是否有 --sft_adapter：
   - 如果有 → 问题是新 eval 提交时漏了参数
   - 如果没有 → 问题是代码变更（inference.py 的 adapter 自动检测逻辑变了）
4. 检查 inference.py 的 --sft_adapter 参数是什么时候加的（git log）

Registry 文件路径: /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_6/projects/faith-evidence-ie/registry.yaml
代码仓库: 在 rtx5090-4x 服务器上 /root/autodl-tmp/freige/

SSH 到 rtx5090-4x 检查:
```
# 检查 inference.py 中 sft_adapter 相关代码
grep -n "sft_adapter" /root/autodl-tmp/freige/freige/eval/inference.py
# git log 看这个参数什么时候加的
cd /root/autodl-tmp/freige && git log --oneline --all -- freige/eval/inference.py | head -10
```

**任务 2: 全面审计**
从 registry.yaml 中提取所有 eval 类实验（exp_id 含 "eval" 或类型为 eval），列出:
- exp_id
- 命令中是否有 --sft_adapter
- 结论 (success/failed/negative/killed)
- rel_f1 值

用表格格式输出审计结果。标注所有可能受影响的 eval。

**产出**:
1. 根因分析报告（是模板问题、手动遗漏、还是代码变更）
2. 全面审计表格
3. 修复建议（如何防止再次发生）

    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

{
  "command": "python3 -c \"\nimport json\nwith open('/home/ubuntu/.claude-account-2/projects/-home-ubuntu--agent-ml-research-idea-gen-0513-6-projects-faith-evidence-ie/790ed0db-98cb-4798-bcfc-16b130feb1ab/tool-results/mcp-agent-ml-research-read_registry-1778881207926.txt') as f:\n    data = json.loads(f.read())\nresult = json.loads(data['result'])\nexps = result['data']['experiments']\n\n# Find all eval experiments\neval_exps = []\nfor eid, exp in exps.items():\n    if 'eval' in eid.lower():\n        spec = exp.get('spec', {})\n        cmd = spec.get('command', '')\n        conclusion = exp.get('conclusion', '')\n        status = exp.get('status', '')\n        metrics = exp.get('metrics', {})\n        rel_f1 = metrics.get('rel_f1', 'N/A') if metrics else 'N/A'\n        has_sft = '--sft_adapter' in cmd if cmd else 'N/A'\n        eval_exps.append({\n            'exp_id': eid,\n            'has_sft_adapter': has_sft,\n            'status': status,\n            'conclusion': conclusion,\n            'rel_f1': rel_f1,\n            'command_snippet': cmd[-200:] if cmd else 'NO_CMD'\n        })\n\nfor e in sorted(eval_exps, key=lambda x: x['exp_id']):\n    print(json.dumps(e, ensure_ascii=False))\nprint(f'\\\\nTotal eval experiments: {len(eval_exps)}')\n\" 2>&1",
  "description": "Parse registry eval experiments"
}

3. **mvp2_eval_rsft_r2b** (rel_f1=0.4493): `has_sft_adapter: false`

These early successful evals didn't use the --sft_adapter flag but still achieved good rel_f1 scores, which suggests the early runs were pointing `--model_path` directly at the RSFT adapter that already had the SFT adapter merged in, or the inference code was auto-detecting it. The --sft_adapter parameter appears to have been introduced later. Now I'm looking at the failing evals to understand what changed.

The unsuccessful runs all have `has_sft_adapter: false` and show near-zero rel_f1 scores, while the recent successful evals both have `has_sft_adapter: true` and achieve rel_f1 around 0.45. This pattern is pretty telling — it looks like the --sft_adapter flag became necessary at some point. Let me trace through the git history of inference.py to see when this parameter was added and how the adapter loading logic evolved.

I'm also noticing that eval_sft_baseline_noquant achieves rel_f1=0.4106 without the flag because it's pointing directly to the SFT adapter output via --model_path, so it doesn't need the separate flag. The older RSFT evals probably worked similarly — they either had the SFT adapter baked into their model_path or the code was automatically loading it first. But now with the new DPO and flat_nli evals, they're also using --model_path to point to their respective outputs, so I need to understand why they suddenly need the --sft_adapter flag to work.

Let me look at the inference.py code to see how adapters are actually being loaded.

自动检测 model_path 是 adapter 还是完整模型\\n    adapter_config = Path(args.model_path) / \\\"adapter_config.json\\\"\\n    if adapter_config.exists():\\n        base_model_path = args.base_model\\n        adapter_path = args.model_path\\n        logger.info(\\\"Detected adapter at %s, base: %s\\\", adapter_path, base_model_path)\\n    else:\\n        base_model_path = args.model_path\\n        adapter_path = None\\n        logger.info(\\\"Full model: %s (no adapter)\\\", base_model_path)\\n\\n    model, tokenizer = load_model_and_tokenizer(\\n        base_model_path, adapter_path, sft_adapter_path=args.sft_adapter,\\n        quantize=args.quantize,\\n    )\\n    _sys_prompt = NO_EVIDENCE_SYSTEM_PROMPT if args.no_evidence else SYSTEM_PROMPT\\n    _include_evi = not args.no_evidence\\n    eval_items, gold, gold_by_doc = prepare_eval_data(\\n        args.data_path, args.split, tokenizer,\\n        include_evidence=_include_evi, system_prompt=_sys_prompt,\\n    )\\n\\n    t0 = time.time()\\n    raw_results = run_inference(\\n        model, tokenizer, eval_items, args.batch_size, args.max_new_tokens\\n    )\\n    elapsed = time.time() - t0\\n    logger.info(\\\"Inference: %.1fs (%.2f s/doc)\\\", elapsed, elapsed / max(len(eval_items), 1))\\n\\n    # 展平为 evaluator 格式\\n    all_predictions = []\\n    raw_outputs_for_eval = []\\n    for r in raw_results:\\n        doc_id = r[\\\"doc_id\\\"]\\n        for triple in r[\\\"parsed_triples\\\"]:\\n            all_predictions.append({\\n                \\\"doc_id\\\": doc_id,\\n                \\\"head\\\": triple[\\\"head\\\"],\\n                \\\"tail\\\": triple[\\\"tail\\\"],\\n                \\\"relation\\\": triple[\\\"relation\\\"],\\n                \\\"evidence\\\": triple.get(\\\"evidence\\\", []),\\n            })\\n        raw_outputs_for_eval.append({\\\"doc_id\\\": doc_id, \\\"raw_text\\\": r[\\\"raw_output\\\"]})\\n\\n    metrics = evaluate(all_predictions, gold, raw_outputs_for_eval, args.data_path, args.split)\\n\\n    n_truncated = sum(1 for r in raw_results if r[\\\"truncated\\\"])\\n    n_format_ok = sum(1 for r in raw_results if r[\\\"format_ok\\\"])\\n    n_total = len(raw_results)\\n    metrics[\\\"n_documents\\\"] = n_total\\n    metrics[\\\"n_predictions\\\"] = len(all_predictions)\\n    metrics[\\\"n_truncated\\\"] = n_truncated\\n    metrics[\\\"n_format_ok\\\"] = n_format_ok\\n    metrics[\\\"format_ok_rate\\\"] = n_format_ok / n_total if n_total else 0.0\\n    metrics[\\\"inference_time_seconds\\\"] = round(elapsed, 1)\\n    metrics[\\\"config\\\"] = vars(args)\\n\\n    # 保存 metrics.json\\n    metrics_path = output_dir / \\\"metrics.json\\\"\\n    with open(metrics_path, \\\"w\\\") as f:\\n        json.dump(metrics, f, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)\\n    logger.info(\\\"Metrics → %s\\\", metrics_path)\\n\\n    # 保存 predictions.json（每文档详细信息）\\n    detailed = []\\n    for r in raw_results:\\n        doc_id = r[\\\"doc_id\\\"]\\n        doc_gold = gold_by_doc.get(doc_id, [])\\n        gold_triples = [\\n            {\\\"head\\\": g[\\\"head\\\"], \\\"tail\\\": g[\\\"tail\\\"], \\\"relation\\\": g[\\\"relation\\\"],\\n             \\\"evidence\\\": g.get(\\\"evidence\\\", [])}\\n            for g in doc_gold\\n        ]\\n        detailed.append({\\n            \\\"doc_id\\\": doc_id,\\n            \\\"raw_output\\\": r[\\\"raw_output\\\"],\\n            \\\"parsed_triples\\\": r[\\\"parsed_triples\\\"],\\n            \\\"gold_triples\\\": gold_triples,\\n            \\\"truncated\\\": r[\\\"truncated\\\"],\\n            \\\"format_ok\\\": r[\\\"format_ok\\\"],\\n        })\\n\\n    pred_path = output_dir / \\\"predictions.json\\\"\\n    with open(pred_path, \\\"w\\\") as f:\\n        json.dump(detailed, f, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)\\n    logger.info(\\\"Predictions → %s\\\", pred_path)\\n\\n    # 控制台汇总\\n    print(\\\"\\\\n\\\" + \\\"=\\\" * 50)\\n    print(f\\\"  F1:          {metrics['f1']:.4f}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"  Ign-F1:      {metrics['ign_f1']:.4f}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"  Evi-F1:      {metrics['evi_f1']:.4f}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"  EDCR:        {metrics.get('edcr', 0):.4f}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"  Format OK:   {n_format_ok}/{n_total} ({metrics['format_ok_rate']:.1%})\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"  Truncated:   {n_truncated}/{n_total}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"  Predictions: {len(all_predictions)}\\\")\\n    print(\\\"=\\\" * 50)\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    main()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.36}}"}

Now I'm seeing the model architecture more clearly. The base Qwen3-4B gets the SFT adapter trained on top of it, which then gets merged to create the foundation for the RSFT, DPO, and GRPO adapters. The inference pipeline needs to load the base model, merge in the SFT adapter, and then load the downstream adapter on top of that merged model. Without the SFT adapter step, those downstream adapters are being applied directly to the raw base model, which explains why they're producing near-zero performance.

Looking back at the earlier successful runs, they either used a model path that was already a merged SFT+RSFT combination, or the RSFT adapter was trained as a standalone model rather than as a second-stage adapter. The mvp2_eval_rsft_r1 command is just a hand-written summary without the actual arguments, so I can't verify what it was doing. But mvp2_eval_sft_baseline shows the pattern—it's explicitly pointing to the SFT output directory with the base model specified separately.

The inference code auto-detects whether model_path contains an adapter by checking for adapter_config.json, then loads it appropriately onto the base model. For the RSFT evaluations, I need to figure out whether the model_path is pointing to a merged checkpoint or an adapter that needs to be loaded on top of the base model. I need to verify whether the rsft_output directories are full merged models or adapters by checking for adapter_config.json files. Let me also look at batch_eval.py to understand how these experiments were actually run. But I think I have enough to piece together what happened—the root cause is that the --sft_adapter parameter was omitted when submitting the eval commands for those three experiments, even though inference.py already supported it.

The evidence is clear: eval_plan_001_flat_nli_s42 with the adapter flag gets rel_f1=0.4539, while s43 without it drops to 0.0022—same experiment, different seed, only difference is the missing flag. The earlier mvp2_eval_rsft_r1 success without the recorded adapter flag was probably either a hand-abbreviated command in the registry or the rsft_output was already a fully merged model at that time. Now I'm checking the audit table to see if the fixed versions of eval_plan_008_topk3 and eval_plan_012_dpo_1ep are already queued up.

I'm also noticing other experiments like eval_plan_012_dpo_ced_ckpt400 that are missing the adapter flag but using DPO outputs—those are likely affected too. Let me pull together the full audit and then verify whether the rsft_output for mvp2_eval_rsft_r1 was a merged model or an adapter by checking the server logs.

_source line 71 · 2026-05-15T21:42:33.863Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
`rsft_output` has `adapter_config.json` — it IS a LoRA adapter. So `mvp2_eval_rsft_r1` with model_path=rsft_output would have triggered the auto-detection (adapter_config.json exists → treat as adapter, use base_model as base).

But wait, mvp2_eval_rsft_r1 succeeded with rel_f1=0.4449 WITHOUT --sft_adapter. How?

Two possibilities:
1. The command recorded in registry is abbreviated (`python -m freige.eval.inference --no-quantize (r1 checkpoint on cuda:2)`) — the actual command may have had --sft_adapter


2. The RSFT adapter was trained on top of a merged SFT model — if the training process merged the SFT adapter into the base first, then trained RSFT LoRA on that merged model, the adapter weights would be deltas relative to the already-SFT-modified base, meaning you'd only need to load the RSFT adapter at inference time without explicitly loading SFT separately.

But that creates a problem: if the early eval loaded the RSFT adapter directly on raw Qwen3-4B without the SFT merge, it would produce garbage outputs. So either the eval code was passing the SFT adapter (just not recorded in the registry), or it had some auto-detection mechanism, or the training actually saved a merged model instead of an adapter. Since rsft_output contains adapter_config.json, it's definitely an adapter, which means the eval code must have been loading SFT somehow — likely the registry command was run manually with the correct parameters even if abbreviated. I should check batch_eval.py to see how other experiments handle the --sft_adapter flag, and also look at what's in the DPO output directory.

I think I have enough to understand the pattern now. The early mvp2 evals were run manually with abbreviated commands where we can't verify the exact flags, but they clearly got correct results. When the plan experiments were submitted programmatically, some included --sft_adapter correctly while others didn't — it's a straightforward parameter omission bug with no code changes involved. Let me verify by checking batch_eval.py as well.
```

```text
Now I have all the information I need. Let me compile the analysis:

Key findings:

1. **batch_eval.py** has `--sft_adapter` as `required=True` (line 80). This means batch_eval.py FORCES the user to provide it. So any eval done via batch_eval.py won't have this issue.

2. **inference.py** has `--sft_adapter` as `default=None` (line 264). This means it's optional in inference.py. When omitted, no SFT adapter is loaded.

3. All output directories (rsft_output, rsft_output_flat_nli, dpo_output_1ep) are LoRA adapters (have adapter_config.json). 

The DPO adapter includes a `ref/` subdirectory with its own adapter configuration that serves as the reference model, but the evaluation code doesn't automatically discover it there. The real issue is that `--sft_adapter` is optional in inference.py with no validation or warnings, so when the eval commands for plan_001_flat_nli_s43, plan_012_dpo_ced_1ep, and plan_008_rsft_topk3 were submitted without this flag, the system proceeded without loading any adapter.

Batch_eval.py avoids this problem by marking `--sft_adapter` as required, and the early mvp2_eval_rsft_r1 likely succeeded because the registry command doesn't reflect the actual executed command. The s42 eval worked because it was submitted with the flag, while s43 failed due to the omission. Now I'm going through the collected data to build a comprehensive audit table of all the eval experiments.

I need to separate them by how they were run: inference.py directly (which needs `--sft_adapter` for non-SFT evals), batch_eval.py (where it's required), and SFT baseline evals where the model path itself is the adapter. The SFT baseline experiments like mvp2_eval_sft_baseline and eval_sft_baseline_noquant don't need the flag since they're testing the SFT output directly, while the RSFT experiments are where the issues arise.

Looking at the RSFT and DPO/GRPO adapter experiments, several are clearly broken without the `--sft_adapter` flag—eval_plan_001_flat_nli_s43, eval_plan_012_dpo_ced_1ep, and eval_plan_008_rsft_topk3 all show either near-zero performance or were killed, while mvp2_eval_rsft_r1 actually worked with rel_f1=0.4449, suggesting it likely had the flag included despite the abbreviated command.

The subsequent mvp2_eval_rsft_r2a and r2b runs confirm this pattern with similar strong performance around 0.449, though r2b_maxtok1536 was killed. Since these early mvp2 runs succeeded, I shouldn't flag them as broken—they probably had the flag or used different code. With the flag properly set, eval_plan_001_flat_nli and plan_011_grpo_g8_bf16_eval both perform well at 0.4539 and 0.4549 respectively.

Now I'm checking the remaining experiments: eval_plan_008_topk3_fixed and eval_plan_012_dpo_1ep_fixed are still preparing, while the newer mvp2 runs show solid results at 0.4504 and 0.4683. Looking at the batch_eval.py experiments, most of them have the required flag or are confirmed to work, so I'm compiling the full picture of what's actually passing. Looking at the checkpoint progression, I'm noticing a pattern where experiments with the --sft_adapter flag show consistently poor rel_f1 scores in the 0.007 to 0.014 range, which is concerning.

Actually, wait—these are GRPO runs with a suboptimal learning rate, so the poor performance makes sense as a legitimate negative result rather than a missing adapter problem. I'm also seeing a couple of other experiments that either lacked the adapter entirely or got killed before completing, but those don't give me clean data to work with. I think I have enough to put together a comprehensive analysis now.

### Assistant

_source line 80 · 2026-05-15T21:44:00.598Z_

数据收集完毕。关键发现：`batch_eval.py` 的 `--sft_adapter` 是 `required=True`，但 `inference.py` 是 `default=None`（可选）。这就是根因。

---

## 根因分析

### 1. 问题机制

`inference.py` 的 adapter 加载链：
```
Qwen3-4B (base) → [merge SFT adapter if --sft_adapter given] → [load RL/RSFT adapter if model_path is adapter]
```

所有 RSFT/DPO/GRPO adapter 都是在 SFT-merged 模型上训练的（它们的权重是相对于 base+SFT 的 delta）。如果跳过 SFT merge 直接把这些 adapter 加载到 raw Qwen3-4B 上，权重错位，输出垃圾，rel_f1≈0。

### 2. 根因：inference.py 的 `--sft_adapter` 是可选参数，无缺失警告

```python
# inference.py line 264
parser.add_argument("--sft_adapter", default=None, ...)
```

而 `batch_eval.py` 做对了：
```python
# batch_eval.py line 80
parser.add_argument("--sft_adapter", required=True, ...)
```

**根因是手动提交 eval 命令时遗漏了 `--sft_adapter` 参数。** 不是代码变更，不是模板问题——是 inference.py 缺少防呆机制。

### 3. 早期 mvp2_eval_rsft_r1 (s42) 为何成功？

registry 中记录的命令是缩写描述（`python -m freige.eval.inference --no-quantize (r1 checkpoint on cuda:2)`），不是实际执行的完整命令。该实验是手动运行的，实际命令大概率包含了 --sft_adapter 或等效参数。rsft_output 确认是 LoRA adapter（有 adapter_config.json），不可能在 raw Qwen3-4B 上拿到 0.4449 的 rel_f1。

### 4. 对比证据

| 实验对 | --sft_adapter | rel_f1 |
|--------|:---:|:---:|
| eval_plan_001_flat_nli (s42) | ✓ | 0.4539 |
| eval_plan_001_flat_nli_s43 | ✗ | 0.0022 |

同一模型，不同 seed，唯一差异就是 `--sft_adapter`。铁证。

---

### 确认受影响的实验（4个）

| exp_id | 应有的 rel_f1 参考值 | 修复状态 |
|--------|:---:|:---:|
| eval_plan_001_flat_nli_s43 | ~0.45 (参考 s42=0.4539) | **需重跑** |

| eval_plan_012_dpo_ced_ckpt400 | 未知 | **需重跑** |

---

## 修复建议

**立即修复：** 在 `inference.py` 中加入防呆逻辑 — 当 model_path 是 adapter 且未指定 --sft_adapter 时，发出警告或报错：

```python
# inference.py main() 中，adapter 检测之后加：
if adapter_path and not args.sft_adapter:
    logger.warning("model_path is a LoRA adapter but --sft_adapter not set. "
                   "Loading adapter on raw base model — this is likely wrong "
                   "for RSFT/DPO/GRPO adapters trained on SFT-merged model.")
```

**防止复发：**
1. `inference.py` 的 `--sft_adapter` 改为在有 adapter 时 required（或至少 loud warning）
2. 提交 eval 时用模板/脚本统一构造命令，不手写
